flask_user/db_adapters/pynamo_db_adapter.py:95:                if getattr(o, k, None).lower() != kwargs[k]:
flask_user/db_adapters/pynamo_db_adapter.py:96:                    break
flask_user/db_adapters/pynamo_db_adapter.py:97:            else:
flask_user/db_adapters/pynamo_db_adapter.py:98:                # all match

flask_user/decorators.py:30:def login_required(view_function):
flask_user/decorators.py:31:    """ This decorator ensures that the current user is logged in.
flask_user/decorators.py:32:
flask_user/decorators.py:33:    Example::
flask_user/decorators.py:34:
flask_user/decorators.py:35:        @route('/member_page')
flask_user/decorators.py:36:        @login_required
flask_user/decorators.py:37:        def member_page():  # User must be logged in
flask_user/decorators.py:38:            ...
flask_user/decorators.py:39:
flask_user/decorators.py:40:    If USER_ENABLE_EMAIL is True and USER_ENABLE_CONFIRM_EMAIL is True,
flask_user/decorators.py:41:    this view decorator also ensures that the user has a confirmed email address.
flask_user/decorators.py:42:
flask_user/decorators.py:43:    | Calls unauthorized_view() when the user is not logged in
flask_user/decorators.py:44:        or when the user has not confirmed their email address.
flask_user/decorators.py:45:    | Calls the decorated view otherwise.
flask_user/decorators.py:46:    """
flask_user/decorators.py:47:    @wraps(view_function)    # Tells debuggers that is is a function wrapper
flask_user/decorators.py:48:    def decorator(*args, **kwargs):
flask_user/decorators.py:49:        user_manager = current_app.user_manager
flask_user/decorators.py:50:        
flask_user/decorators.py:51:        # User must be logged in with a confirmed email address
flask_user/decorators.py:52:        allowed = _is_logged_in_with_confirmed_email(user_manager)
flask_user/decorators.py:53:        if not allowed:
flask_user/decorators.py:54:            # Redirect to unauthenticated page
flask_user/decorators.py:55:            return user_manager.unauthenticated_view()
flask_user/decorators.py:56:
flask_user/decorators.py:57:        # It's OK to call the view
flask_user/decorators.py:58:        return view_function(*args, **kwargs)
flask_user/decorators.py:59:
flask_user/decorators.py:60:    return decorator

flask_user/decorators.py:63:def roles_accepted(*role_names):
flask_user/decorators.py:64:    """| This decorator ensures that the current user is logged in,
flask_user/decorators.py:65:    | and has *at least one* of the specified roles (OR operation).
flask_user/decorators.py:66:
flask_user/decorators.py:67:    Example::
flask_user/decorators.py:68:
flask_user/decorators.py:69:        @route('/edit_article')
flask_user/decorators.py:70:        @roles_accepted('Writer', 'Editor')
flask_user/decorators.py:71:        def edit_article():  # User must be 'Writer' OR 'Editor'
flask_user/decorators.py:72:            ...
flask_user/decorators.py:73:
flask_user/decorators.py:74:    | Calls unauthenticated_view() when the user is not logged in
flask_user/decorators.py:75:        or when user has not confirmed their email address.
flask_user/decorators.py:76:    | Calls unauthorized_view() when the user does not have the required roles.
flask_user/decorators.py:77:    | Calls the decorated view otherwise.
flask_user/decorators.py:78:    """
flask_user/decorators.py:79:    # convert the list to a list containing that list.
flask_user/decorators.py:80:    # Because roles_required(a, b) requires A AND B
flask_user/decorators.py:81:    # while roles_required([a, b]) requires A OR B
flask_user/decorators.py:82:    def wrapper(view_function):
flask_user/decorators.py:83:
flask_user/decorators.py:84:        @wraps(view_function)    # Tells debuggers that is is a function wrapper
flask_user/decorators.py:85:        def decorator(*args, **kwargs):
flask_user/decorators.py:86:            user_manager = current_app.user_manager
flask_user/decorators.py:87:
flask_user/decorators.py:88:            # User must be logged in with a confirmed email address
flask_user/decorators.py:89:            allowed = _is_logged_in_with_confirmed_email(user_manager)
flask_user/decorators.py:90:            if not allowed:
flask_user/decorators.py:91:                # Redirect to unauthenticated page
flask_user/decorators.py:92:                return user_manager.unauthenticated_view()
flask_user/decorators.py:93:
flask_user/decorators.py:94:            # User must have the required roles
flask_user/decorators.py:95:            # NB: roles_required would call has_roles(*role_names): ('A', 'B') --> ('A', 'B')
flask_user/decorators.py:96:            # But: roles_accepted must call has_roles(role_names):  ('A', 'B') --< (('A', 'B'),)
flask_user/decorators.py:97:            if not current_user.has_roles(role_names):
flask_user/decorators.py:98:                # Redirect to the unauthorized page
flask_user/decorators.py:99:                return user_manager.unauthorized_view()
flask_user/decorators.py:100:
flask_user/decorators.py:101:            # It's OK to call the view
flask_user/decorators.py:102:            return view_function(*args, **kwargs)
flask_user/decorators.py:103:
flask_user/decorators.py:104:        return decorator
flask_user/decorators.py:105:

flask_user/decorators.py:151:def allow_unconfirmed_email(view_function):
flask_user/decorators.py:152:    """ This decorator ensures that the user is logged in,
flask_user/decorators.py:153:    but allows users with or without a confirmed email addresses
flask_user/decorators.py:154:    to access this particular view.
flask_user/decorators.py:155:
flask_user/decorators.py:156:    It works in tandem with the ``USER_ALLOW_LOGIN_WITHOUT_CONFIRMED_EMAIL=True`` setting.
flask_user/decorators.py:157:
flask_user/decorators.py:158:    .. caution::
flask_user/decorators.py:159:
flask_user/decorators.py:160:        | Use ``USER_ALLOW_LOGIN_WITHOUT_CONFIRMED_EMAIL=True`` and
flask_user/decorators.py:161:            ``@allow_unconfirmed_email`` with caution,
flask_user/decorators.py:162:            as they relax security requirements.
flask_user/decorators.py:163:        | Make sure that decorated views **never call other views directly**.
flask_user/decorators.py:164:            Allways use ``redirect()`` to ensure proper view protection.
flask_user/decorators.py:165:
flask_user/decorators.py:166:
flask_user/decorators.py:167:    Example::
flask_user/decorators.py:168:
flask_user/decorators.py:169:        @route('/show_promotion')
flask_user/decorators.py:170:        @allow_unconfirmed_emails

flask_user/tests/tst_utils.py:14:def response_has_string(response, string):
flask_user/tests/tst_utils.py:15:    assert response.status_code == 200

flask_user/token_manager.py:31:
flask_user/token_manager.py:32:        Fernet is basically AES128 in CBC mode, with a timestamp and a signature.
flask_user/token_manager.py:33:

flask_user/token_manager.py:61:
flask_user/token_manager.py:62:        Note: strings may not contain any ``'|'`` characters, nor start with a ``'~'`` character

flask_user/token_manager.py:81:
flask_user/token_manager.py:82:        | Returns None if token is expired or invalid.

flask_user/token_manager.py:116:        """Timestamp, sign and encrypt a string into a token using ``cryptography.fernet.Fernet()``."""
flask_user/token_manager.py:117:
flask_user/token_manager.py:118:        # Convert string to bytes
flask_user/token_manager.py:119:        concatenated_bytes = concatenated_str.encode()
flask_user/token_manager.py:120:
flask_user/token_manager.py:121:        # Encrypt, timestamp, sign, and base64-encode

flask_user/token_manager.py:123:
flask_user/token_manager.py:124:        # Convert bytes to string

flask_user/token_manager.py:131:    def decrypt_string(self, token_str, expiration_in_seconds=None):
flask_user/token_manager.py:132:        """Verify signature, verify timestamp, and decrypt a token using ``cryptography.fernet.Fernet()``."""
flask_user/token_manager.py:133:
flask_user/token_manager.py:134:        # Add '=' padding if needed
flask_user/token_manager.py:135:        if len(token_str) % 4:
flask_user/token_manager.py:136:            token_str += '=' * (4 - len(token_str) % 4)
flask_user/token_manager.py:137:
flask_user/token_manager.py:138:        # Convert string to bytes
flask_user/token_manager.py:139:        encrypted_bytes = token_str.encode()
flask_user/token_manager.py:140:
flask_user/token_manager.py:141:        # Verify signature, verify expiration, and decrypt using ``cryptography.fernet.Fernet()``

flask_user/token_manager.py:145:        return concatenated_str

flask_user/token_manager.py:147:    def encode_data_items(self, *args):
flask_user/token_manager.py:148:        """ Encodes a list of integers and strings into a concatenated string.
flask_user/token_manager.py:149:
flask_user/token_manager.py:150:        - encode string items as-is.
flask_user/token_manager.py:151:        - encode integer items as base-64 with a ``'~'`` prefix.
flask_user/token_manager.py:152:        - concatenate encoded items with a ``'|'`` separator.
flask_user/token_manager.py:153:
flask_user/token_manager.py:154:        Example:
flask_user/token_manager.py:155:            ``encode_data_items('abc', 123, 'xyz')`` returns ``'abc|~B7|xyz'``
flask_user/token_manager.py:156:        """
flask_user/token_manager.py:157:        str_list = []
flask_user/token_manager.py:158:        for arg in args:
flask_user/token_manager.py:159:
flask_user/token_manager.py:160:            # encode string items as-is
flask_user/token_manager.py:161:            if isinstance(arg, str):
flask_user/token_manager.py:162:                arg_str = arg
flask_user/token_manager.py:163:
flask_user/token_manager.py:164:            # encode integer items as base-64 strings with a '~' character in front
flask_user/token_manager.py:165:            elif isinstance(arg, int):
flask_user/token_manager.py:166:                arg_str = self.INTEGER_PREFIX + self.encode_int(arg)
flask_user/token_manager.py:167:
flask_user/token_manager.py:168:            # convert other types to string
flask_user/token_manager.py:169:            else:
flask_user/token_manager.py:170:                arg_str = str(arg)
flask_user/token_manager.py:171:
flask_user/token_manager.py:172:            str_list.append(arg_str)
flask_user/token_manager.py:173:
flask_user/token_manager.py:174:        # Concatenate strings with '|' separators
flask_user/token_manager.py:175:        concatenated_str = self.SEPARATOR.join(str_list)
flask_user/token_manager.py:176:
flask_user/token_manager.py:177:        return concatenated_str
flask_user/token_manager.py:178:
flask_user/token_manager.py:179:    def decode_data_items(self, concatenated_str):
flask_user/token_manager.py:180:        """Decodes a concatenated string into a list of integers and strings.
flask_user/token_manager.py:181:
flask_user/token_manager.py:182:        Example:
flask_user/token_manager.py:183:            ``decode_data_items('abc|~B7|xyz')`` returns ``['abc', 123, 'xyz']``
flask_user/token_manager.py:184:        """
flask_user/token_manager.py:185:
flask_user/token_manager.py:186:        data_items = []
flask_user/token_manager.py:187:        str_list = concatenated_str.split(self.SEPARATOR)
flask_user/token_manager.py:188:        for str in str_list:
flask_user/token_manager.py:189:
flask_user/token_manager.py:190:            # '~base-64-strings' are decoded into integers.
flask_user/token_manager.py:191:            if len(str)>=1 and str[0]==self.INTEGER_PREFIX:
flask_user/token_manager.py:192:                item = self.decode_int(str[1:])
flask_user/token_manager.py:193:
flask_user/token_manager.py:194:            # Strings are decoded as-is.
flask_user/token_manager.py:195:            else:
flask_user/token_manager.py:196:                item = str
flask_user/token_manager.py:197:
flask_user/token_manager.py:198:            data_items.append(item)
flask_user/token_manager.py:199:
flask_user/token_manager.py:200:        # Return list of data items
flask_user/token_manager.py:201:        return data_items
flask_user/token_manager.py:202:
flask_user/token_manager.py:203:    def encode_int(self, n):
flask_user/token_manager.py:204:        """ Encodes an integer into a short Base64 string.
flask_user/token_manager.py:205:
flask_user/token_manager.py:206:        Example:
flask_user/token_manager.py:207:            ``encode_int(123)`` returns ``'B7'``.
flask_user/token_manager.py:208:        """
flask_user/token_manager.py:209:        str = []
flask_user/token_manager.py:210:        while True:
flask_user/token_manager.py:211:            n, r = divmod(n, self.BASE)
flask_user/token_manager.py:212:            str.append(self.ALPHABET[r])
flask_user/token_manager.py:213:            if n == 0: break
flask_user/token_manager.py:214:        return ''.join(reversed(str))
flask_user/token_manager.py:215:
flask_user/token_manager.py:216:    def decode_int(self, str):
flask_user/token_manager.py:217:        """ Decodes a short Base64 string into an integer.
flask_user/token_manager.py:218:

flask_user/user_mixin.py:60:        """ Return True if the user has all of the specified roles. Return False otherwise.
flask_user/user_mixin.py:61:
flask_user/user_mixin.py:62:            has_roles() accepts a list of requirements:
flask_user/user_mixin.py:63:                has_role(requirement1, requirement2, requirement3).
flask_user/user_mixin.py:64:
flask_user/user_mixin.py:65:            Each requirement is either a role_name, or a tuple_of_role_names.
flask_user/user_mixin.py:66:                role_name example:   'manager'
flask_user/user_mixin.py:67:                tuple_of_role_names: ('funny', 'witty', 'hilarious')
flask_user/user_mixin.py:68:            A role_name-requirement is accepted when the user has this role.
flask_user/user_mixin.py:69:            A tuple_of_role_names-requirement is accepted when the user has ONE of these roles.
flask_user/user_mixin.py:70:            has_roles() returns true if ALL of the requirements have been accepted.
flask_user/user_mixin.py:71:
flask_user/user_mixin.py:72:            For example:
flask_user/user_mixin.py:73:                has_roles('a', ('b', 'c'), d)
flask_user/user_mixin.py:74:            Translates to:
flask_user/user_mixin.py:75:                User has role 'a' AND (role 'b' OR role 'c') AND role 'd'"""
flask_user/user_mixin.py:76:
flask_user/user_mixin.py:77:        # Translates a list of role objects to a list of role_names
flask_user/user_mixin.py:78:        user_manager = current_app.user_manager

flask_user/user_mixin.py:92:                if not authorized:
flask_user/user_mixin.py:93:                    return False                    # tuple_of_role_names requirement failed: return False
flask_user/user_mixin.py:94:            else:
